Restoring the real call restored a pass on all 16 tests in `send_message_live_postgres.rs`. Separately, the fifteenth instance of this file's own repeated `hasRole('system')` gap — `Procedures::ensure_consent_on_file` reads `ConsentRecord` under `sys` from the moment it was written, so it was classified straight into `SYSTEM_READABLE_MODELS` (never shipped broken, same "flagged in advance" shape as `Route`/`User`/`Role`), and pulling `hasRole('system')` from its `read` clause reproduced:

```
a system context could not read back the ConsentRecord row it (or an owner, on its behalf)
just seeded. ConsentRecord's current schema.cstack read/list/detail policy is:
@@allow("read", auth().kind == "user" || appId == auth().appId || hasRole('system')).
```

before being restored.

**No `@@retain` on `ConsentRecord`, and this is a real, unresolved tension flagged rather than solved.** `Message` purges `msisdn` at 90 days because minimisation applies to content with no remaining purpose (#67) — but proof of consent is not that; it's the thing you produce *because* you were challenged, which argues for keeping it at least as long as the relationship lasts, plausibly longer. Keeping `msisdn` in plaintext indefinitely to serve that need reproduces the exact problem #67 closed for `Message`. Not resolved here. `msisdnHash` — what the consent check actually matches on — inherits the same pepper-rotation-orphans-purged-rows caveat every other `msisdnHash` column in this schema already has (§2.5/§3.1, `sms_api::pepper`'s module doc).

**Schema regeneration, verified the documented way.** `/tmp/cs710/bin/cratestack --version` confirmed `0.7.10` against the `=0.7.10` pin before regenerating (the machine's global CLI had drifted to `0.7.12`). `cratestack migrate diff` against the changed schema produced exactly one new table (`consent_records`, two `CHECK` constraints for `scope`/`channel`, one `FOREIGN KEY` to `apps`) and nothing else — confirmed by diffing the output against the committed `0001_init` before copying it over. `0002_bootstrap` regenerated from the doc (`ci/gen-bootstrap-sql.py`, `ConsentRecord` added to that script's own model list so it picks up the standard `id`/`created_at`/`updated_at` defaults and `touch_updated_at` trigger the same as every other `@use(Timestamps)` model, plus one hand-written lookup index, `consent_records_lookup_idx (app_id, msisdn_hash, scope)`, mirroring the reasoning every other hand-written index in that section already documents). Both migrations applied to a real, disposable Postgres 16 (`ci/apply-migrations.sh`), `ci/test-state-machine.sql` passed unchanged, and a manual insert/read/CHECK-violation probe against the real table confirmed the defaults and constraints behave as written before either file was trusted. `sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust/schema.cstack` re-vendored and its own `cargo check --all-targets` confirmed clean, the exact step whose omission made #185 red for a prior PR.

Explicit scope cuts, named rather than left implicit: no `recordConsent` procedure — `ConsentRecord` is created via plain generated CRUD, mirroring `OptOut`'s own exact create-policy shape (`hasRole('owner') || hasRole('admin') || hasRole('operator') || hasRole('system')`, no `auth().kind == "app"`) and its own exact same limitation (no app-level self-service write path exists for either model yet — an app's own signup backend recording consent the instant a user opts in is the obvious future caller, the same status quo gap `OptOut` already has for inbound STOP). No Layer 2 scope literals added for `ConsentRecord` — unlike `Job`/`Provider`/`Route`, this model carries `appId` and its `read` policy already scopes machine callers to `appId == auth().appId` at Layer 1, the same shape `Message`'s own read policy already uses without a Layer 2 gate on top.

## Milestone 4: the console authenticates upstream as the human (#211) — landed 2026-08-13

#194 built the mechanism (a real `authorization_code` + PKCE login, `GatewayAuth` resolving a genuine human principal); nothing used it. `frontends/packages/gateway` kept authenticating **every** upstream call as the console's own machine credential (`SMS_CONSOLE_CLIENT_ID`, `role: "app"` always) regardless of who was signed in — found live, signing in as a freshly provisioned `owner` and watching a `Provider` edit fail with `missing required permission "provider:update"`. That made #52/#58's own write screens buildable-but-pointless (every edit would 403 on an otherwise-correct Layer 2 check) and left `@@audit` unable to say *who* made a console write.

**The decision, and why it was nearly free.** Two shapes were possible: forward the human's own `accessToken` and let `GatewayAuth` validate it directly, or exchange the session for a separate per-request token. Checked before assuming: `GatewayAuth::authenticate_human` (`backends/crates/sms-api/src/auth.rs`) already fully validates a human token — signature via JWKS, issuer, and audience against `sms-console` (`human_client_id`) — and `frontends/apps/admin/middleware.ts` already refreshes `session.accessToken` ~60s ahead of expiry on *every* request, redirecting to `/login` outright if refresh fails. So by the time a request reaches the tRPC route handler, the token is already fresh for that request's lifetime. No exchange step, no expiry logic needed in `frontends/packages/gateway` at all — forwarding the session token verbatim was correct and, per the issue's own framing, "nearly free."

**The plumbing: `AsyncLocalStorage`, not a threaded parameter — and why that shape specifically.** `frontends/packages/gateway`'s ~13 upstream-calling functions across 9 files (`rest.ts`, `providers.ts`, `routes.ts`, `route-simulator.ts`, `workers.ts`, `dashboard.ts`, `jobs.ts`, `messages.ts`, `client.ts`) are called via `ctx.gateway.xxx()` in `frontends/packages/api/src/routers/*.ts` — a reference to the whole module, not a per-request-bound instance. Threading an explicit credential parameter would have touched every one of those call sites *and* every router. Instead, `frontends/packages/gateway/src/request-credential.ts`'s `AsyncLocalStorage<RequestCredential>` is set exactly once, at the single true per-request boundary — `frontends/apps/admin/app/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts`'s `handler`, reading the `x-vsms-access-token` header `frontends/apps/admin/middleware.ts` forwards (same mechanism already established for `x-vsms-actor`/`x-vsms-role`, just carrying a real bearer token this time) — and every ordinary call site resolves it implicitly via `resolveUpstreamAccessToken()`.

**The property the ticket actually asked for — impossible for a new screen to silently get the machine credential — is structural, not a convention to remember.** `resolveUpstreamAccessToken()` throws if no credential scope was ever entered, rather than defaulting to the machine credential. A new gateway function gets the human token automatically the moment it's invoked through the ordinary tRPC path (which always sets one). Reaching for the machine credential requires importing `getMachineAccessToken` from `./token` directly, by name — grep-able, and reserved by that module's own doc for exactly two documented exceptions:

- **`client.ts`'s `previewMessage`/`sendMessage`.** `backends/crates/sms-api/src/procedures.rs::Procedures::caller_client_id` hard-rejects any caller whose `kind` isn't `"app"` — "sendMessage currently requires a machine (client_credentials) caller — deriving an App for a human caller has no design yet." This is a pre-existing Rust-side constraint, not a choice #211 made: forwarding a human token here wouldn't merely be the wrong credential, it would turn every composer send into a guaranteed `Validation` error.
- **`messages.ts`'s `listMessagesForStream`.** `MessageStreamHub` is a process-wide singleton — one poll loop shared by every open browser tab, triggered by whichever request happens to call `subscribe()` first. `AsyncLocalStorage` context set up inside a `.run()` call propagates to timers/callbacks scheduled during that call's lifetime, indefinitely — so if this function resolved the ambient credential, the *first* operator to open the Messages tab would have their own token captured into `hub.start()`'s `setInterval`, and every other operator's tab would silently poll using that first operator's credential. `messages.ts::getJsonWith` takes `resolveToken`/`onUnauthorized` as parameters specifically so `getJson` (human, used by `listMessages`/`getMessageById`/`listMessageReceipts`) and `getJsonAsMachine` (used only by `listMessagesForStream`) can share one fetch/retry implementation while never sharing a token source.

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed in the same PR, because forwarding a human token for the first time is what finally exercised it.** The seeded role `permissions` (`backends/migrations/postgres/0002_bootstrap`, source of truth `docs/architecture.md` §2.10) used `message:read`/`message:send` where `backends/crates/sms-api/src/rbac.rs::require_permission` actually checks the literals `sms:read`/`sms:send` — the same ones the service-account scope table already used correctly (`SMS_CONSOLE_SCOPE=sms:send sms:read ...`). And no role carried `dashboard:read` at all, despite §5.2's own prose table naming it for `operator`. Both were silent because no human token had ever reached `sms-api` before #211 to hit `listMessageReceipts`/`dashboardSummary`'s own `require_permission` checks. Fixed by renaming the seed literals (`message:read`→`sms:read`, `message:send`→`sms:send`) and adding `dashboard:read` to `owner`/`admin`/`operator`/`auditor` — data-only, no schema/DDL change (`permissions` is a plain `String` column; `cratestack migrate diff` untouched). This is the same recurring shape this file has now recorded many times over (a policy gap invisible until the right context actually exercises it), just on seeded data instead of a `@@allow` clause.

**A real, deliberate consequence, not a bug: cross-app visibility becomes live for the first time.** `Message`/`DeliveryReceipt`'s own `@@allow` already admitted `auth().kind == "user"` unconditionally — unscoped by `appId` — before this PR (`schema.cstack`, unchanged by #211). Nothing had ever exercised it, because no human token reached `sms-api`. Forwarding one means the Messages list and Dashboard now show *every app* in this deployment to *any* signed-in human, regardless of role — genuinely wider visibility than the machine-credential-scoped-to-one-app behaviour before it, and exactly the "cross-app visibility" the issue named as something it unblocks for #50. The messages list's own live-update poll (`onStateChange`) stays scoped to the machine credential's one app, deliberately (see `listMessagesForStream`'s own exception above) — so a row belonging to another app can appear in the initial list but won't receive a live update until the next refetch. `messages-screen.tsx`'s own module doc and on-screen banner state this; it is not silently smoothed over. `providers-screen.tsx`, `routes-screen.tsx`, and `dashboard-screen.tsx`'s own stale "cannot succeed / scoped to this app only" banners (written before #211, when they were true) were rewritten to match reality rather than left to rot — the exact "documentation asserts something the code does not do" shape this file has flagged repeatedly, caught here before merge rather than found live later.

**Proven live end to end**, not just reasoned about, via `just demo` (`docs/runbooks/getting-started.adoc`'s own login flow): signed in as `demo@vsms.local` (`--role-key owner`), a `Provider` edit that previously 403'd now succeeds — `Provider.displayName` and `version` genuinely changed in Postgres, and `cratestack_audit`'s own `actor` column reads `{"sub": "<demo@vsms.local's real User.id>", "kind": "user", "role": "owner", ...}`, not the machine `AppClient`'s `client_id` — the audit trail can now say *who*. A second user provisioned with `--role-key auditor` (which carries `provider:read` but not `provider:update`) gets a real, live `403` — `Save failed: missing required permission "provider:update"` — on the identical edit, and the row is confirmed unchanged in Postgres afterward. `frontends/packages/gateway/src/request-credential.test.ts`'s own fail-loud guard was broken on purpose (made `resolveUpstreamAccessToken()` silently return the machine token instead of throwing when no scope was entered) and confirmed to fail with `promise resolved 'machine-token' instead of rejecting` before being restored.

**One same-origin trap hit while proving this live, worth recording so it isn't rediscovered:** `frontends/packages/api/src/context.ts`'s pre-existing CSRF check (`assertSameOriginForMutations`, unrelated to #211, untouched by it) compares the request's `Origin` header against `new URL(req.url).origin`. Reaching the dev server via `http://127.0.0.1:3100` in a browser-automation environment produced a genuine mismatch (`req.url`'s computed origin came back `http://localhost:3100` even though the browser's own `window.location.origin` and `Origin` header both correctly said `127.0.0.1`) — GET requests were unaffected (the check only runs on POST), so reads worked throughout, but the mutation 403'd until the whole flow (login redirect_uri included) was redone consistently through `http://localhost:3100`. Not a bug this PR introduced or needed to fix; recorded so the next person debugging a "read works, write 403s with a cross-origin message" symptom in a similar sandboxed setup doesn't waste time suspecting the credential-forwarding mechanism first.

## Milestone 4: Sender IDs and Webhooks landed (#53/#55) — landed 2026-08-13

Two more of the four screens #211 unblocked. Paired deliberately, in one PR — both are per-record status surfaces over procedures that already existed (`rotateWebhookSecret`/`replayWebhookAttempt`, #41/#43; `SenderId`/`SenderIdRegistration` generated CRUD) — so this story is almost entirely exposing backend work rather than inventing it, the same shape #56/#57 had for `Job`/worker locks.

**`router::SENDER_AND_WEBHOOK_WRITE_ROUTES`** is `PROVIDER_WRITE_ROUTES`'s own defense-in-depth shape (#24), applied to `SenderId`/`SenderIdRegistration` (`sender:manage`) and `WebhookEndpoint` (`webhook:manage`) create/update/delete routes — none of the three models' write `@@allow` admits `auth().kind == "app"`, so Layer 1 alone already restricts every one of these routes to a human role. Unlike `PROVIDER_WRITE_ROUTES`'s own doc, which had to describe a hypothetical future because no human-role token existed when it was written, this gate is real and provable *today*: #194/#211 both landed first, so a signed-in `owner` genuinely reaches these routes end to end — proven live (`just demo`, real `owner` session): a real `PATCH /sender_id_registrations/{id}` moved a registration `approved -> rejected` with a real `rejectionReason`, `version` bumped; a real `POST /$procs/rotateWebhookSecret` minted a fresh secret and moved the old one to `prevSecret`. **Guard-failure proof, this PR's own new code**: `sender_write_route_denies_a_token_with_no_scope_at_all` (`backends/crates/sms-auth/tests/rbac_layer2_live_postgres.rs`) with the `POST /sender_ids` entry temporarily removed from the gate's own route list still returned `403` (Layer 1 alone still refuses a machine-credential token), but with a different, generic message — `"create policy denied this operation"` instead of naming `sender:manage` — proving the test genuinely exercises Layer 2, not just any denial. Restored, reconfirmed passing.

**`WebhookEndpoint.secret`/`prevSecret` are displayed, not hidden — a deliberate call, not an oversight.** `WebhookEndpoint.read`'s `@@allow` (`owner`/`admin`/`developer`/`system`, #187) is the only real control; `@sensitive` redacts audit snapshots only, per this file's own §2.0 entry, so a real `GET` by any of those three roles returns the live plaintext secret every time — not once at creation, the way `provisionAppClient`'s private key or `provision-user`'s printed password are. `webhooks-screen.tsx` masks it in the UI (default `whsec_••••••••••<last 4>`, explicit "Reveal"/"Copy") as a screen-share/shoulder-surf discipline, not a pretend security boundary — withholding data that already crossed the wire to an authorized, authenticated session would be theatre, not a control. Creation needed a caller-supplied secret (`WebhookEndpoint.secret` has no `@default`) — generated here (`crypto.randomBytes(32)`, `whsec_<64 hex>`, matching `sms_webhook::generate_secret()`'s own format byte-for-byte) rather than left for an operator to type, with a one-time "copy it now" banner on both create and rotate — honest about not being a true one-time secret (see above), just the newest, safest moment to grab it.

**A real, previously-undiscovered framework gap, found live while proving the registration-edit write path, not assumed:** JSON `null` **cannot clear a nullable field over a generated `PATCH` route** — a verified no-op, indistinguishable from omitting the key. This directly contradicts this file's own "Verified toolchain API" section's `Option<Option<T>>` claim, which is accurate only for the Rust delegate builder (`.set(UpdateXInput { field: Some(None), .. })`); the generated REST JSON deserializer applies no disambiguation. Read directly from the vendored source, not guessed: `cratestack-macros-0.7.10/src/model/struct_only.rs::struct_field_definition` wraps every update-input field in a plain `Option<#base_type>` for the patch case with no `#[serde(deserialize_with = ...)]` anywhere near it — the textbook serde "double Option" ambiguity (a present `null` and an absent key both collapse to the *outer* `None` via serde-derive's built-in "missing `Option` field defaults to `None`" convenience, since the outer `Option<T>::deserialize` sees `null`, calls `visit_none()`, and returns without ever touching the inner `Option`). `update_sql_value` (`cratestack-macros-0.7.10/src/shared/sql.rs`) then reads that outer `None` as "field not present in this update" and skips it — indistinguishable from the field never having been sent. Verified live, not just read off the source: `PATCH /sender_id_registrations/{id}` with `{"status": "pending", "rejectionReason": null}` updated `status` normally while `rejectionReason` stayed completely unchanged in the same request — proof the route itself works and that only the null-clear semantics don't. No existing caller in this codebase (`providers.ts`/`routes.ts`) had ever attempted to clear a nullable field over REST before this PR, so nothing had surfaced it. Filed upstream as [cratestack#567](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/issues/567) and added to `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` §4. Worked around locally, not upstream: `SenderId.notes`/`SenderIdRegistration.reference`/`rejectionReason` treat an explicit empty string as "clear" — a present, non-null value the route genuinely writes — folded back to `undefined` on read (`normalizeSenderId`/`normalizeRegistration`, `@vsms/gateway/senders.ts`) so a round-trip read can't tell a never-set column apart from one this screen cleared. Safe only because nothing in `backends/crates/sms-api` queries or branches on `NULL`-ness for either column — the next model that needs to clear a *queried-on* nullable column over REST needs the real upstream fix, not this trick. **A second guard-failure proof, this PR's own new code, targeting the identical class of bug on the `eventTypes` packing side**: `packEventTypes([])` must equal `sms_core::pack`'s own `EMPTY` constant (`" "`, one space) exactly — a naive `` ` ${arr.join(" ")} ` `` implementation instead produces `"  "` (two spaces) for an empty selection. `frontends/packages/gateway/src/webhooks.test.ts`'s own assertion was broken on purpose (swapped in that naive implementation) and failed with `expected '  ' to be ' '` before being restored.

**Attempt history is real, not a happy-path screenshot — proven with a genuine failure in it, per the story's own evidence bar.** No `hooks`-role worker runs in `just demo`'s own default process set (`dispatch,scheduler,jobs` only), so a one-off `sms-worker --roles hooks` was pointed at the same demo database (a second `--metrics-listen` port to avoid colliding with the demo's own worker) against a webhook endpoint deliberately created pointing at an unreachable `http://127.0.0.1:9/...` — two real `failed` attempts landed, `last_error` carrying a genuine `error sending request for url (...)` from a real refused connection, both `AttemptStatusPill`-rendered as "Failed (retrying)" with working `Replay` buttons. Replaying one via `POST /$procs/replayWebhookAttempt` reset it to `pending`/`attempts: 0` with a fresh `nextAttemptAt`, and the returned `payload` confirmed `maskRecipient` survives the round trip verbatim (`"to":"+237*******87"`) — the masking is baked in at subscriber time (#38) and this screen never touches it, exactly as designed.

**`AttemptStatusPill`** (`frontends/packages/ui/src/components/status/attempt-status-pill.tsx`) is a third status-pill component, mirroring `JobStatusPill`'s own precedent rather than widening either existing one: `AttemptState`'s `failed` is retryable (same shape `JobState.failed` already has, styled `unresolved`/`uncertain` not terminal `danger`), and collapsing three state machines into one prop type would blur exactly the distinction each one's own transitions table draws. `ATTEMPT_STATUS_META` added to `status-tokens.ts` alongside `JOB_STATUS_META`.

**Explicit scope cuts, named rather than left implicit:** no App picker for `WebhookEndpoint.appId` on create — a plain text ID input, the same "no App CRUD screen exists yet" shape `routes-screen.tsx`'s own `matchAppId` field already has; `WebhookEndpoint.secret`/`prevSecret`/`consecutiveFailures`/`circuitOpenUntil` stay out of the edit form entirely (rotation is the only path this screen offers for the secret, matching the audited-procedure-over-plain-field-edit precedent `rotateWebhookSecret` itself already sets); no Layer 2 read gate for any of the four new list/detail routes — unlike `Job`/`Provider`/`Route`, none of these four models' `read` `@@allow` admits `auth().kind == "app"` unscoped, so a signed-in human's own `kind == "user"` already clears Layer 1 without a machine-credential path for Layer 2 to be the real perimeter for.

## `frontends/packages/gateway`: one null-normalization seam replaces fourteen (#221) — landed 2026-08-13

The exact bug this file's own #54/#50/#52-#58 sections each record finding once — sms-api sends an explicit JSON `null` for a nullable column with no value, never an omitted key, and every TypeScript type in this package assumed the opposite (`field?: T | undefined`) — had been fixed three separate times, each with its own local `normalize*` function next to whichever screen happened to render the one field that exposed it. By the time this PR started counting, that was fourteen near-identical functions across twelve files (`routes.ts`, `providers.ts`, `route-simulator.ts`, `app-clients.ts`, `apps.ts`, `roles.ts`, `dashboard.ts`, `opt-outs.ts`, `users.ts`, `senders.ts` ×2, `webhooks.ts` ×2), not the five the issue was filed against — #52/#58 alone added five more after the issue's own count was taken. Two modules, `jobs.ts` and `workers.ts`, had never been caught at all: `JobRecord.dedupeKey`/`leaseOwner`/`leaseUntil`/`lastError`/`startedAt`/`finishedAt` and `WorkerLockInfo.workerId`/`pid`/`heldSince` are exactly the same shape of nullable column, silently unguarded, latent until someone happened to drive the Jobs or Workers screen through a case that renders one.

**`frontends/packages/gateway/src/json.ts` is now the single seam — option (1) from the issue's own three, taken as written.** Option (2), adopting the generated `frontends/packages/sms-client` (T3), remains the thing that would stop this *class* of bug recurring for good — `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` §5 still tracks it, unchanged by this PR — but it's blocked on an upstream cratestack release and out of scope here. Option (3), changing the server to omit nulls, was rejected for the same reason the issue itself gives: a breaking API change to fix a client-side assumption, when other consumers may reasonably prefer explicit nulls.

**The hazard the issue asked to be checked before writing a line of code, and the actual mechanism chosen.** A blind recursive `null -> undefined` strip over every response would silently corrupt data if any response legitimately carries a JSON value where `null` is meaningful — most obviously a stored JSON payload forwarded byte-for-byte. Checked directly against `schemas/vsms.cstack`, not assumed: every field in this codebase that carries free-form or pre-serialised JSON — `WebhookAttempt.payload` (the signed envelope's `data`, forwarded exactly as stored per `hooks.rs`'s own doc — AGENTS.md's own #40 section is explicit that what gets signed must be exactly what gets sent), `cratestack_audit`'s `actor`/`before`/`after`/`primaryKey` snapshot columns (`audit_log.rs`), and `Provider.config` — is declared a plain `String`, never a structured type. sms-api therefore always sends each as a JSON *string* value, and `JSON.parse` on the outer response turns every one into a single opaque string leaf; `normalizeValue`'s recursion only ever descends into a plain object or an array, never into a string's own contents, so these fields are structurally unreachable regardless of what they encode — including a payload whose encoded text contains the literal substring `"null"`. `RawCallback`/`DeliveryReceipt.rawPayload` were checked too and found moot: neither field ever reaches this console at all (`DeliveryReceiptSummary`, `messages.ts`, deliberately omits `rawPayload`). That structural fact is the primary guarantee; `VERBATIM_STRING_FIELDS` (`payload`/`actor`/`before`/`after`/`primaryKey`/`config`) is a second, named guard on top of it, defence in depth for if a future schema change ever turned one of these columns into a genuinely structured type — costs nothing today since skipping recursion into a value that's already a string leaf is a no-op.

**The paged envelope (`{ items, totalCount, pageInfo }`, only `@@paged` models) and the bare array (`Provider`/`Route`, per #54's own finding) both fall out of the same generic walk with no special-casing** — an envelope is just an object whose `items` value happens to be an array of objects, and a bare array is just the top-level value being an array instead of an object; `normalizeValue` doesn't need to know which shape it's looking at. `pageInfo.offset: number | null` normalizes to `undefined` along with everything else — grepped before writing this section, and confirmed nothing anywhere in this codebase reads it, so there was nothing to preserve.

**Every per-module `normalize*` was deleted, not deprecated** — this repo's own standing "hard cutover, not a parallel path" convention. Two exceptions, kept because they are a *different* concern from #221 and would be actively wrong to fold into the generic seam: `senders.ts`'s `foldClearedSentinel` (renamed from the null-folding half of `normalizeSenderId`/`normalizeRegistration`) still folds an explicit `""` back to `undefined` — that's #53/#55's own workaround for cratestack#567 (a generated `PATCH` route cannot distinguish a `null` from an absent key, so this module writes `""` to mean "clear"), unrelated to what `null` means on a `GET`. `webhooks.ts`'s `normalizeEndpoint` still unpacks `eventTypes` from its sentinel-packed wire string into an array — model-specific shape logic, not null-handling.

**Guard-failure proof, `frontends/packages/gateway/src/json.test.ts`.** `normalizeValue`'s body was temporarily replaced with a bare `return value;` (a no-op) — 10 of 13 tests failed immediately, each with the exact `null` the fix exists to convert showing up where `undefined` was expected (`expected undefined, received null` on a top-level field, a nested field two levels into a `SimulateRouteResult`-shaped fixture, an array element, and the paged-envelope fixture); the three that kept passing were the ones asserting *non*-null values pass through untouched and the parse-failure/empty-body cases, which the sabotage never touched. Restored, all 13 passed again. Thirteen tests cover: top-level null, nested null one and two levels deep, null inside a plain-value array, a bare top-level null, the paged envelope, and — the adversarial case the hazard analysis above exists to justify — a `payload`/`actor`/`before`/`after`/`config` string whose own encoded text contains the literal substring `null` twice, asserted byte-for-byte untouched.

**A retroactive fix, not just a refactor: `jobs.ts` and `workers.ts` never had a `normalize*` at all, so this PR is the first thing that has ever guarded their nullable fields.** Neither module's own doc previously said so; both now carry a note explaining why the absence of a `normalize*` function was itself an instance of the bug, not evidence of a clean bill of health — matching this file's own "a golden test that has never failed hasn't proven anything" standard applied one level up, to a missing test's missing subject.

`messages.ts`'s own `MessageRecord` doc comment — the one the issue itself quotes as the source of `messages-screen.tsx`'s wrong assumption ("a message with no `stateReason` simply has no `stateReason` key... all of them are, confirmed live") — is corrected in the same PR: that claim was checked live for exactly one field and stated as general, which is precisely what let `submittedAt: null` through in #50. `frontends/apps/admin/app/messages/[id]/timeline.ts`'s own doc, which had named `@vsms/gateway`'s wrong type as "a separate, pre-existing issue, not fixed in this PR," is updated to say it's fixed now — `buildTimeline`'s own `| null` type and loose `!= null` check are deliberately left in place regardless, since the function's contract shouldn't depend on trusting one particular upstream package, and `timeline.test.ts`'s `submittedAt: null` case exercises it directly, bypassing `@vsms/gateway` entirely.

## Repo automation moved to `xtask` — every `ci/*.sh`/`ci/*.py` check is now `cargo xtask <check>`

The maintainer's standing direction: no bash (or Python) script survives in this repo — `xtask` plus Docker, for full portability. `.xtask/` is a new workspace member (a dot-directory at the repo root, invoked via the `[alias] xtask` in `.cargo/config.toml`), mirroring the sibling `vpay` repo's own xtask shape — one module per check (`raw_sqlx.rs`, `parity.rs`, `bootstrap_sql.rs`, `sdk_schema.rs`, `migrations_current.rs`, `cratestack_pin.rs`, plus a shared `diff.rs`), so a future subcommand only ever conflicts with the `match` arm in `main.rs`. Deliberately depends on nothing that expands `include_server_schema!` — no `sms-api`, no `cratestack` — so `cargo xtask <check>` stays fast (a cold `cargo build -p xtask` takes under 3s, pulling in only `regex` and its own transitive deps, none of which needed a fresh download since `regex` was already resolved in `Cargo.lock` via other crates). Six scripts were ported and deleted in the same PR — `ci/assert-no-raw-sqlx.sh`, `ci/assert-state-machine-parity.py`, `ci/gen-bootstrap-sql.py`, `ci/assert-sdk-schema-current.sh`, `ci/assert-migrations-current.sh`, `ci/cratestack-pin.sh`, and `sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust/vendor-schema.sh` — a hard cutover, no bash/Python left calling into a parallel Rust path.

**`bootstrap_sql.rs` (`cargo xtask bootstrap-sql`) is the highest-risk port — its output IS the committed `0002_bootstrap/up.sql`, applied to real databases — and was verified, not assumed, byte-for-byte identical to the deleted Python's real output before being trusted.** The Python's own header text names itself (`-- Generated ... by ci/gen-bootstrap-sql.py.`), which the port cannot literally reproduce forever without leaving a stale self-reference in a committed migration the moment that file is deleted — exactly the "documentation asserts something the code does not do" pattern this file has flagged many times elsewhere. So verification ran in two passes: first with the Rust port's `HEADER` constant temporarily edited to match the Python's exact wording, confirming a **byte-for-byte, SHA-256-identical** output (`diff -u` empty, hashes equal) — proof the port's actual generation logic (section slicing, fenced-block extraction and rejoining, the two placeholder replacements, table pluralisation for all 19 `@use(Timestamps)` models) has no bug; then with `HEADER` restored to name `cargo xtask bootstrap-sql` instead, which is the one, reviewed, intentional line of drift the committed migration now carries relative to its pre-PR self. The module's own doc comment carries this same two-pass proof inline so the next reader doesn't mistake the one-line diff for a regression.

**Porting `raw_sqlx.rs` (`cargo xtask no-raw-sqlx`) surfaced a real, pre-existing disagreement between the enforced allowlist and `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own R1 exceptions table — not invented for this PR.** The shipped script's allowlist had always included `backends/crates/sms-test-support/src/lib.rs` (it takes `pg_advisory_lock`/`pg_advisory_unlock` and issues `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE`/`DROP DATABASE ... WITH (FORCE)` against the live-test harness's own per-binary scratch databases — advisory locks and database-catalog DDL, the same two categories two existing rows already cover), but `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s table never had a matching row, so the reasoning for an enforced exception was undocumented. Fixed by adding the row (see `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s R1 table) rather than silently carrying the gap forward into the port — the same "cross-check both sides, reconcile the disagreement" instruction this PR was built against. `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s exceptions table is now 12 rows (previously described as complete at a stale count in its own closing sentence, which is corrected too).

**Two deliberate simplifications versus the deleted bash, neither behavioural:** `migrations_current.rs` lets the `cratestack migrate diff` child process's stdout/stderr inherit directly to this process's own streams, rather than replicating the original script's `cmd >&2` trick of routing the child's stdout specifically onto file descriptor 2 — nothing anywhere parses that script's own stdout (it was never captured by a caller), so the distinction is cosmetic log-stream separation, not a behaviour a caller depends on. `diff.rs`'s `line_diff` is a plain positional line-by-line comparison, not a real LCS-based diff — sufficient for both callers (each compares a generator's own fresh output against its own prior output, so a genuine drift is almost always a contiguous changed block) and avoids a dependency on the system `diff` binary being present on whatever runner this ever executes on, at the cost of a noisier hunk when a single line is inserted or removed in the middle of a long, mostly-unchanged file (confirmed live: inserting one column into `App` produced a cascade of `-`/`+` pairs for every subsequent, merely-shifted line in `migrations-current`'s own guard-failure proof below — still correctly named the file as drifted and gave regeneration instructions, just a less readable hunk than `diff -u` would show).

**Every guard was proven to actually fail, not just asserted to pass — the house standard this file applies everywhere else, applied here to the checks themselves for the first time.** `cargo xtask no-raw-sqlx`: a temporary `cratestack::sqlx::query!("SELECT 1")` added to `backends/crates/sms-api/src/procedures.rs` (a non-allowlisted file) was caught by name and line number, then reverted. `cargo xtask parity`: deleting the `queued --> routed: claimed by dispatch` line from §7.4's own mermaid diagram reproduced `queued -> routed: in the table, missing from the diagram`; separately, adding a `('failed','queued')` row to the doc's own `message_state_transitions` `INSERT` (then regenerating `0002_bootstrap`) reproduced both `failed -> queued: in the table, missing from the diagram` **and** a terminal-states mismatch (`table [...,"failed",...] vs diagram [...]` without it) — a stronger result than the brief asked for, since the terminal-state check is a second, independent assertion inside the same function. `cargo xtask migrations-current`: with the pinned `=0.7.10` CLI on `PATH`, adding a throwaway `xtaskDriftProbe String?` field to `App` in `schemas/vsms.cstack` reproduced a real drift failure naming the migration as stale, with regeneration instructions; removing it restored a pass. All three sabotages were reverted before this PR's own diff was finalised — `git diff --stat` confirmed clean on every touched file afterward.

**`0002_bootstrap/up.sql` and `0001_init` were also verified the way `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own "Changing the schema" workflow prescribes, not just regenerated and trusted:** the regenerated `0002_bootstrap/up.sql` (one line changed — the header, above) was applied to a real, disposable Postgres 16 container (`ci/apply-migrations.sh`, unmodified) and `ci/test-state-machine.sql` passed in full (`ALL ASSERTIONS PASSED`) before being committed.

Deliberately **not** touched, per the brief: `ci/apply-migrations.sh`, `scripts/`, `deploy/`, `backends/crates/sms-test-support/`, and the justfile's `test-live`/`test-live-clean` recipes — all owned by other in-flight work. `docs/roadmap.md` was checked and needs no edit: this PR is infrastructure/tooling, not a milestone completion, blocker resolution, dependency change, or infrastructure-ahead-of-its-milestone landing per that file's own four update triggers.

## The `xtask` PR's own "deliberately not touched" list, finished — `containerize-tooling`

The maintainer's direction went further: no bash script survives *anywhere* in this repo, not just in `ci/`, and beyond that — nothing runs on a developer's host at all; the actual work happens in pinned containers, driven by Compose. `scripts/demo.sh`, `scripts/e2e-integration.sh`, `ci/apply-migrations.sh`, and `deploy/{backup,restore,restore-drill,backup-entrypoint}.sh` — the seven scripts the xtask PR above named and explicitly deferred — are gone. `git ls-files | grep -E '\.(sh|py)$'` now returns nothing, repo-wide.

**Where the line actually got drawn, stated plainly rather than left implicit:** `cargo xtask`/`just`/`docker compose` themselves still run on the host — that's the same accepted exception the xtask PR above already established for `cargo xtask <check>`, extended here to the two thin orchestrators (`just demo`, `just e2e-integration`) that sequence a handful of `docker compose`/`docker` calls. What changed is that every *application* process — Postgres, migrate, sms-gateway, sms-worker, sms-fake-orange, admin, and now backup/restore/the e2e verification tool — runs in a container built from a pinned base image, and the tooling that used to be bash is now either a real Rust binary or a declarative compose file, never both a script and a container wrapping it.

- **`ci/apply-migrations.sh` is simply deleted, not ported.** `app/sms-migrate` already was its Rust equivalent (`AGENTS.md`'s own "rustls, musl, and distroless" section, months earlier) — every caller (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `migrations` job, `justfile`'s `schema-check`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own documented workflow, two runbooks) now runs `cargo run -p sms-migrate` directly. The CI job change is a real, if incidental, strengthening: it now proves the actual production migration binary applies cleanly to an empty database, not a parallel shell-script path that could silently drift from it — the exact "two copies of one concept, only one gets updated" shape this file warns about elsewhere.
- **`scripts/demo.sh` (300 lines: `docker run` by hand, backgrounded host processes, `pnpm exec next dev`, hand-parsed subcommand stdout) became `compose.dev.yaml`** — a third full-stack compose file, deliberately not reusing `compose.yml` (considered and rejected: Compose's multi-file merge semantics for list-typed keys like `ports` were more fragile to verify than a six-line duplicated `postgres:` service, and `compose.demo.yaml` had already independently made the identical call) and structured to mirror `compose.demo.yaml`'s own shape (a chain of one-shot seed/provision services, `--profile console` for R4) with `build:` in place of `image:`. `just demo` is now `docker compose -f compose.dev.yaml --profile console down -v` then `up -d --wait` — no host Rust/Node build step for the running system at all (a developer's own `cargo build`/`pnpm install` for *editing* code is unaffected, obviously; this is about what `just demo` itself does).
- **A real, reproduced BuildKit hazard, not theoretical:** every `app/*/Dockerfile` builder stage deliberately shares one cache-mount id (`cargo-registry-musl`) "so building any one warms the cache for the other three" (their own comment) — true for sequential builds, and a genuine race (`failed to unpack package ...: File exists (os error 17)`) for `docker compose build`'s default parallelism building all of them uncached for the first time at once. Reproduced twice, gone entirely under `COMPOSE_PARALLEL_LIMIT=1`, which `just demo`'s own recipe now sets for its build step. A second, independent finding from the same investigation: giving all seven `sms-gateway`-image services in `compose.dev.yaml` the *same* explicit `image:` tag (rather than Compose's default of one distinct image per service, even for byte-identical `build:` blocks) was necessary — without it, two builds from identical `context`+`dockerfile` intermittently produced different tagged images on this machine, one of them stale (missing a subcommand the source genuinely had).
  **Corrected 2026-08-15, found live running `just demo` on a different machine, not from a PR:** `COMPOSE_PARALLEL_LIMIT=1` alone stopped closing this race the moment Compose defaults `build` to `buildx bake` (confirmed: Docker Compose `v5.3.1`/buildx `v0.35.0`) — bake fans every target out into one concurrent invocation regardless of that env var, which only ever throttled the legacy per-service build loop. The identical race reproduced on this machine, a different crate each time (`pem`, then `pkcs1` on retry), with `COMPOSE_PARALLEL_LIMIT=1` set exactly as originally documented; `COMPOSE_BAKE=false` alone on top of it wasn't sufficient either (`ps` still showed simultaneous `rustc` processes across multiple image targets). Fixed for real, not just documented: `just demo`'s own recipe now forces strictly sequential per-image builds — see the justfile's own comment directly above `demo:` for the full mechanism and the six-distinct-image dedup list. Verified end to end after the change (`docker compose ... ps` — every service healthy on the first try, no manual workaround), not just reasoned about.
- **`scripts/e2e-integration.sh` (bash + `openssl dgst -sign` + `curl` + `jq`, hand-rolling RFC 7523 client-assertion signing) became `ci/e2e-integration`**, a small standalone Rust binary (its own Cargo workspace, like `examples/rust`/`sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust` — invisible to `cargo check --workspace` by construction) reusing `vsms-sdk-rust`'s own `private_key_jwt` token exchange rather than reimplementing it a second time. **Found live, not assumed, while building it: `VsmsClient::private_key_jwt(base_url, config)`'s own doc comment claims `base_url` backs both the OIDC `/token` endpoint and the REST API — false.** `PrivateKeyJwtTokenStore::new` (`sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust/src/token.rs`) derives `token_endpoint` exclusively from `config.issuer`; `base_url` only ever reaches the generated REST client. Confirmed with a real `401 invalid_client` from the real `/token` route when the two values differ. This is the identical "documentation asserts something the code does not do" failure shape this file has now recorded many times over vsms itself, this time in the SDK — flagged as a spawned follow-up rather than fixed here (`sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust` is outside this PR's own file ownership). The practical consequence: `ci/e2e-integration` cannot run as a bare host process against `compose.dev.yaml` the way `examples/rust/sms-send` is designed to run against a real, single-address deployment — `sms-gateway`'s own configured issuer has to be the internal Compose DNS name for `admin`'s server-side OIDC calls to work at all, and a host process can't reach that name. Fixed structurally, not worked around: `ci/e2e-integration` gets its own Dockerfile and runs as a one-shot container joined to `compose.dev.yaml`'s own network, where the address it connects to and the gateway's own configured identity are simply the same string.
- **`deploy/{backup,restore,restore-drill,backup-entrypoint}.sh` became `deploy/backup-tool`** (`vsms-backup`, another standalone crate, its own Cargo workspace under `deploy/` since `app/` was out of this PR's ownership) — `backup`/`restore`/`restore-drill`/`schedule` subcommands. `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`/`rclone` stay external processes on purpose (the same reasoning `app/sms-migrate`'s own module doc already gives for *not* reimplementing `psql`'s protocol — these are real binary tools nobody reimplements), but every ad-hoc `psql -Atc` query became a typed `postgres::Client` call (`db.rs`), the JSON manifest became `serde`-typed, and Alpine's busybox `crond` + a hand-written `/etc/crontabs/root` line became an in-process cron-expression scheduler (`cron` crate) with its own `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` handling — found live while designing it, not assumed: a process that is PID 1 *within its own PID namespace* (which this container's entrypoint always is) is kernel-exempted from a signal's *default* disposition unless it installs a handler, so an unhandled `SIGTERM` would have been silently ignored rather than terminating the process, and `docker stop` would have hung for the full grace period every time. The runtime image lost `bash` and `openssl` entirely (the restore-drill fallback pepper is `rand`-generated Rust now) — only `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`/`rclone` remain as external binaries. `docs/runbooks/backup-restore.adoc`'s own restore drill was re-run for real against this rewrite (a real, network-isolated Postgres 16, migrated by `app/sms-migrate`, a real domain row, a *named* `rclone` remote rather than the bare-local-path shortcut) and passed — table-by-table exact row counts, the marker row, the pepper-fingerprint warn-not-block behaviour, and retention pruning, all re-verified rather than assumed carried over from the deleted bash version's own results. **A guard-failure proof was run for this rewrite too:** `pgtools::restore_custom_format` was temporarily made a no-op, and the drill failed — not with the row-count mismatch the check was written to catch, but one step earlier and louder, a hard `relation "public.backup_drill_marker" does not exist` error, because the schema the destructive step had just dropped was never restored at all. A stronger result than the check was designed to produce, recorded as such rather than silently claiming only the intended path fired. Reverted, rebuilt (confirmed byte-identical image manifest to the pre-sabotage build), re-run clean.

## cratestack bumped to `=0.7.16` (from `=0.7.10`)

`v0.7.10...v0.7.16` (compare: [cratestack/cratestack](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/compare/v0.7.10...v0.7.16)) is six releases, 75 commits, several breaking. This is not the "checked and found inert" shape the 0.7.10 bump section above mostly was — three real breaking changes reach this repo's Rust code directly, one of them exposing genuine, previously-invisible bugs in this repo's own live-test fixtures, and a fourth reached `frontends/packages/gateway`, found and fixed live against a real gateway rather than patched around blind. **The isolated CLI matched the pin at every step** (`/tmp/cs716/bin/cratestack --version` → `0.7.16`), never the machine's global install (drifted to `0.7.12` at the time).

**DDL is unchanged.** `cratestack migrate diff` (isolated `=0.7.16` CLI) against the unmodified `schemas/vsms.cstack` diffed byte-for-byte identical to the committed `0001_init/{up,down}.sql` — confirmed with `diff -u`, not eyeballed. Migrations were left untouched, per this file's own standing rule. **MSRV is unchanged** — `cratestack`'s own root `Cargo.toml` at `v0.7.16` still declares `rust-version = "1.95.0"`, matching this repo's own floor; no toolchain bump needed.

### Breaking changes that reach this repo, each with the fix

- **cratestack#512 (0.7.13, PR [#540](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/540)): every `ProcedureRegistry` method gained an unconstructible witness parameter.** Before this, `registry.<procedure>(&db, &ctx, args)` (three arguments) compiled and **silently skipped every `@allow`/`@deny`/`@authorize` check** — a real, upstream-confirmed bug. Now every generated trait method takes a fourth parameter, `_authorized: <procedure>::Authorized`, a private zero-sized type constructible only inside that procedure's own generated module, by `authorize_with_db`/`invoke_with_db`. `backends/crates/sms-api/src/procedures.rs`'s `impl ProcedureRegistry for Procedures` block gained the parameter on all 18 methods (ignored, mechanical). Every internal caller that used to call the trait method directly — `backends/apps/sms-gateway/src/main.rs`'s `provision-client` CLI command, and eleven live-test files across `backends/crates/sms-api/tests/`, `backends/crates/sms-auth/tests/`, `backends/apps/sms-gateway/tests/`, plus `backends/crates/sms-api/examples/send_test_message.rs` — now goes through `<procedure>::invoke_with_db(&db, &args, &ctx, |authorized| registry.<procedure>(&db, &ctx, args.clone(), authorized))`, the "sanctioned way to invoke a procedure from non-HTTP code" per that function's own doc comment. `provision-client`'s own pre-existing `--role` validation (`"owner"`/`"admin"` only, matching `provisionAppClient`'s `@allow` exactly) meant this was a pure no-op behaviourally there; the live-test call sites needed the identical mechanical fix.

- **The same fix landing everywhere it needed to exposed a second, deeper effect of the same bug: `@authorize(Model, action, args.path)` was never actually enforced by any of this repo's own direct-call tests, at any prior cratestack version.** Four vsms procedures carry `@authorize` (`schema.cstack`): `cancelMessage` (a `not_yet(...)` stub, no test coverage, unaffected), `replayWebhookAttempt`, `requeueJob`, `listMessageReceipts`. `authorize_with_db` — now genuinely reachable — runs `db.<model>().authorize_detail(id, ctx)` (`cratestack-sqlx-0.7.16/src/delegate/model_authorize.rs`), a real `SELECT 1 FROM <table> WHERE id = $1 AND <detail policy>` preflight, **before the procedure body ever runs**. This closed a real, previously-invisible test-fixture bug and forced a second, genuine finding:
  - **`backends/crates/sms-api/tests/list_message_receipts_live_postgres.rs`'s `app_caller_with_sms_read()`/`app_caller_without_sms_read()` had always constructed their `CratestackContext` with a hardcoded empty `app_id`, never the seeded message's real one.** `Message.detail`'s own `@@allow` (`auth().kind == "user" || appId == auth().appId || hasRole('system')`) has always required the two to match for a `kind == "app"` caller — but since the direct-call bypass meant this clause was never actually evaluated by these tests, the mismatch was invisible since the test file was first written. Once `@authorize` started running for real, all three tests in that file failed with `Forbidden("detail policy denied this operation")` — including the happy-path test seeding a real, owned message. Fixed at the root: both helper functions now take `app_id: &str` and every caller passes the real seeded id.
  - **A nonexistent id can no longer produce `NotFound` for `replayWebhookAttempt`/`requeueJob`, ever, for any caller — this is real, permanent, verified production behaviour, not a test artifact.** The `SELECT 1 ... WHERE id = $1 AND <policy>` preflight cannot distinguish "no row" from "row exists but policy denies" — the exact ambiguity `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s own R1 section already documents for `CratestackError::Forbidden` on update/delete, now reachable from a procedure's own `@authorize` preflight too. `replaying_an_unknown_attempt_id_is_not_found`/`requeuing_an_unknown_job_id_is_not_found` (renamed `..._is_refused`) now assert `Forbidden`, with a doc comment explaining the mechanism and recording that reverting the assertion to `NotFound` was confirmed, live, to reproduce the old failure — the procedure's own internal `NotFound`-producing lookup is unreachable for a missing id now, live only in the (vanishingly unlikely) TOCTOU window between the preflight and the procedure body's own read.
  - **The two "Layer 2 denies a caller missing the specific permission" tests (`replay_denies_a_caller_with_no_webhook_manage_permission`, `requeue_denies_a_caller_with_no_job_enqueue_scope`) pointed at a nonexistent id too — which now means Layer 1's `@authorize` preflight denies first, every time, regardless of the caller's actual Layer 2 permissions, so the test stopped proving what its own name claims (the denial message no longer named the missing permission at all).** Fixed by seeding a real, existing row the caller's `kind` already satisfies at Layer 1 (`WebhookAttempt.detail`/`Job.detail` both admit `kind == "user"`/`"app"` unconditionally, per each model's own `@@allow`), so Layer 2's `require_permission` is what genuinely produces the denial again. Each fix's own doc comment records the exact failure reproduced with the old nonexistent-id shape, confirmed live before restoring.
  - All three files (10 tests total) pass, confirmed against a real Postgres, not assumed from reading the mechanism.

- **cratestack#534 (0.7.13, PR [#554](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/554)): every `run_in_tx` write builder now returns `RunInTxOutcome<T>` (the value plus any `AuditEvent`s built for a caller-installed `AuditSink`), not a bare `T`.** This repo installs no `AuditSink` — `@@audit` rows land in `cratestack_audit` inside the same transaction exactly as before, unaffected — so the entire fix is mechanical: every `.run_in_tx(&mut tx, ctx).await?` whose return value is read (not just `?`-discarded) in `backends/crates/sms-api/src/procedures.rs` gained a trailing `.value` (six call sites: `provisionAppClient`'s `AppClient` create, `rotateWebhookSecret`'s `WebhookEndpoint` update, `replayWebhookAttempt`'s `WebhookAttempt` update, `requeueJob`'s `Job` update, `provisionConsoleUser`'s `User` create). Calls whose result is never read needed no change.

- **cratestack#552 (0.7.13, PR [#552](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/552)): `RequestContext<'_>` gained a mandatory `extensions: &'a http::Extensions` field**, so an `AuthProvider` can see whatever a preceding tower/axum layer inserted (`ConnectInfo`, an mTLS peer identity, …) — the intent is real but nothing in `GatewayAuth::authenticate` reads it yet. Four hand-built `RequestContext` construction sites needed a value regardless: `backends/crates/sms-api/src/rbac.rs`'s `enforce_route_permission`, `backends/crates/sms-api/src/router.rs`'s `verify_idempotency_principal` (both clone the live request's own `.extensions()` — the same value the generated dispatch path would see for that request), and two unit tests in `backends/crates/sms-api/src/auth.rs` (a fresh empty `http::Extensions::new()`, since nothing in either test reads it).

- **cratestack#413 (0.7.12, PR [#522](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/522)): the generated `router()`/`rpc_router()` gained a real, required `body_limit_bytes: usize` constructor parameter** — a request-body cap that used to be axum's own implicit, unnamed 2 MiB `Bytes`-extractor default, now explicit. `backends/crates/sms-api/src/router.rs`'s one call site passes `cratestack::DEFAULT_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES` (re-exported from `cratestack-core::limits`, the identical 2 MiB figure) — provably a no-op on this upgrade, not a new restriction, and correct regardless: no message body, webhook payload, or provider response this system handles is remotely close to 2 MiB.

- **cratestack#519 (0.7.13, PR [#538](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/538)): `DELETE` on an `@version` model now enforces `If-Match` exactly like `PATCH` already did** — omitting it returns `412 PreconditionFailed` (`cratestack-sqlx-0.7.16/src/query/write/delete.rs`: `if self.descriptor.version_column.is_some() && self.if_match.is_none() { return Err(PreconditionFailed(...)) }`, checked before the delete query runs at all, independent of `@@soft_delete`). Ten models carry `@version` (#59: `App`, `AppClient`, `SenderId`, `SenderIdRegistration`, `Provider`, `Route`, `OperatorPrefixRule`, `WebhookEndpoint`, `User`, `Role`). **Fixed on the Rust side** — every `.delete(id)` call in the live-test suite on a `@version`d model gained `.if_match(...)` (`backends/crates/sms-api/tests/policy_golden_list_live_postgres.rs`'s `Provider`/`AppClient` delete-policy golden tests, `backends/crates/sms-api/tests/simulate_route_live_postgres.rs`'s `Route` cleanup loop, `backends/apps/sms-gateway/tests/login_flow_live_postgres.rs`'s `ReservedRoleFixture::cleanup`, the latter needing a fresh `find_unique` re-read since the fixture never threaded a version through). **Also fixed on the TypeScript side, in `frontends/packages/gateway`** — see "A real regression, found and fixed" below.

- **The generated `invoke_with_db` doc comment (new in 0.7.13, spliced into every procedure module alongside the `Authorized` witness) broke this repo's own standard live-suite command — and broke CI, in a way the first pass at this section's own local verification missed.** Its illustrative example is deliberately non-compiling pseudocode, correctly fenced `` ```ignore `` (referencing `SystemContext`/`registry`/`db` names that don't exist in that scope — by design, since it's prose, not a real call site) — a plain `cargo test` genuinely skips it, reported `ignored`, not a failure. The break is `--ignored`'s own second, unrelated meaning: rustdoc reuses the identical "ignored" bucket for both "a real test, temporarily skipped, run it when explicitly asked" (what `--ignored` is meant to force-run) and "a doc example marked non-compiling on purpose" (which `--ignored` then also force-compiles, with nothing to tell the two apart). `cargo test --workspace -- --ignored` (this repo's own documented live-suite command, and CI's own `live-Postgres suites` job) genuinely tries to compile that pseudocode once per procedure (18 failures, `cannot find value/type` for names the example never defines) and fails the whole run.

  **What the first pass at this fix actually verified, and why it wasn't enough:** `just test-live` was changed to `cargo test --workspace --tests -- --ignored`, restricting to the `tests/` target kind (no live-Postgres suite is ever a doctest) — a real, correct fix for the *local* convenience command, confirmed clean. But CI's own job runs the bare command, no `--tests`, and that command was never re-run locally before the first push — the gap this whole section exists to record. **Caught in CI, not locally, and the fix corrected once found**: `backends/crates/sms-api/Cargo.toml` now carries `[lib] doctest = false`, with an inline comment giving the real justification (not "doctests were noisy") — this crate's entire public surface is macro-generated from `schemas/vsms.cstack`, so its doc comments are upstream's own illustrative text, not this repository's documentation, and doctesting them tests cratestack's comment text, not this repo's code. Checked before disabling, not assumed: `backends/crates/sms-api/src/`'s own hand-written source has exactly one fenced code block (`procedures.rs`, a ` ```text ` block, never compiled as a doctest regardless) — zero genuine hand-written doctests exist in this crate to lose. No sibling crate needed the same fix: `cratestack::include_server_schema!` is invoked exactly once in this workspace (`backends/crates/sms-api/src/lib.rs`) — every other file matching `include_server_schema!` in a grep is prose referencing the macro in a doc comment, not an invocation, confirmed by reading each one. Filed upstream as a real, reproducible defect, not a local workaround: [cratestack#611](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/issues/611) — any consumer whose own CI runs `cargo test -- --ignored` in the crate hosting `include_server_schema!` hits the identical collision, with no schema or code change of their own able to fix it. **Superseded 2026-08-23: cratestack#611 is fixed in 0.8.6, and `[lib] doctest = false` has been removed from `backends/crates/sms-api/Cargo.toml` along with the comment block described here — see the `=0.8.10` bump section above for the three-way verification and the live proof. `just test-live`'s own `--tests` is gone with it — that recipe is now CI's command plus `--no-fail-fast` (so a local run reports every failing suite rather than stopping at the first), with the target-kind restriction removed, which is exactly what this paragraph argues should have been true all along. The paragraph is kept as written because the reasoning it records — why `--tests` was never the real fix, and why CI's own bare command is the one that must be run — is the durable lesson; only the two workarounds are gone.**

  **Verified against the exact commands that must pass, not a proxy for them:** `cargo test --workspace -- --ignored` (no `--tests` — CI's own command) and plain `cargo test --workspace` (no `--ignored`) were both re-run to completion after the fix, matching the standing lesson `#87` already established for this file — when a local run and CI disagree, the fix is to run what CI runs, not to trust that a related command passing is close enough.

### A real regression, found and fixed: DELETE from the admin console

`frontends/packages/gateway/src/rest.ts`'s `deleteResource` (and the hand-rolled equivalents that used to live in `apps.ts`/`users.ts`/`roles.ts`) sent no `If-Match` header at all. Every caller — `deleteRoute`, `deleteWebhookEndpoint`, `deleteApp`, `deleteUser`, `deleteRole` — targets a `@version`'d model, so every one of these delete buttons would genuinely 412 against a real cratestack 0.7.16 gateway. `frontends/apps/admin/`, `frontends/packages/ui/`, and `docs/design/` were excluded from this PR's scope — `frontends/packages/gateway` was not, and that is exactly where the fix belongs: `deleteResource` now acquires the row's current `ETag` via an internal `GET` (reusing `fetchWithEtag`) before issuing the `DELETE` with it as `If-Match` — **the function's own signature is unchanged**, so every existing call site (`deleteRoute`, `deleteWebhookEndpoint`, and `deleteApp`/`deleteUser`/`deleteRole`, the latter three now simply delegating to `deleteResource` instead of duplicating the same request a second time) keeps working with zero screen changes, nothing to collide with the console redesign in flight under `frontends/apps/admin/`.

**Honest about the cost, not just the fix.** There is a real TOCTOU window between the internal `GET` and the `DELETE`: a row edited by another operator in that window makes the captured `ETag` stale, and the `DELETE` gets a genuine `412` — that is `If-Match` doing exactly its job, not a bug, but it can fail a delete that would have succeeded moments earlier. A caller that already holds the row's current `version` (every screen does, since it necessarily rendered the row to offer a delete button) could pass it straight through and skip both the round trip and the race — named in `deleteResource`'s own doc comment as the better long-term shape for Phase 2 of the console redesign, needing a signature change and per-screen wiring this interim deliberately doesn't attempt.

**Verified live against a real gateway (`just demo`), both directions, not assumed from reading the mechanism.** A temporary vitest script (not committed) logged in as the demo's own provisioned operator through the real `authorization_code` + PKCE flow, then called the real `deleteResource` inside a real `runWithRequestCredential` scope against the real running `sms-gateway`:

- **Positive case**: created a throwaway `Route`, called `deleteResource` — returned successfully, no throw, no `412`; a follow-up `GET` confirmed a real `404` (the row is actually gone).
- **Negative case**: created a second throwaway `Route`, captured its `ETag` (`"0"`), then a genuine concurrent `PATCH` bumped its version to `1` — simulating the exact TOCTOU race the doc comment names — and a `DELETE` carrying the now-stale `"0"` `ETag` came back `412 PRECONDITION_FAILED`, `"version mismatch: expected 0, found 1"`, straight from the real server. Proof the header is genuinely being honoured, not silently ignored.

`frontends/packages/gateway/src/rest.test.ts` gained five unit tests for `deleteResource` covering the same two shapes at the mock level (acquire-and-send, no-version-no-header, 404-on-the-GET-still-attempts-the-DELETE, a stale-`If-Match` `GatewayError` recognisable via `isStaleWriteError`, and the 401-retry-on-the-DELETE-itself case) — 52 tests total in the package, all passing; `tsc --noEmit` and `biome check` both clean.

### The known-version fast path, the thing the section above named as future work, built the same day

`deleteResource`'s own doc comment already named the better long-term shape and deliberately didn't build it — "needing a signature change... which is exactly the work the console redesign already in flight should do for Phase 2, not a reason to leave deletes broken today." That work landed the same day, not deferred again: `deleteResource(path, routeLabel, etag?, fetcher?)` — an optional `etag` inserted before `fetcher`, so every existing call site (none of which ever passed a `fetcher` explicitly outside `rest.test.ts`) keeps compiling unchanged. When `etag` is supplied, `deleteResource` sends it straight onto the `DELETE` as `If-Match` and never calls `fetchWithEtag` at all — one request, no TOCTOU window. When omitted, the pre-existing `GET`-first fallback still runs, so a caller with no version handy keeps working exactly as before.

**Every screen already had the version in state — none needed new plumbing to reach it.** `routes-screen.tsx`/`webhooks-screen.tsx`'s `deleteTarget` is a row pulled straight from the list query (`RouteListItem`/`WebhookEndpointRecord`, both carry `version`, same value `onSubmit`'s own `updateMutation` call already synthesizes an `ETag` from); `apps-screen.tsx`/`users-screen.tsx`'s `AppDetailDrawer`/`UserDetailDrawer`/`RoleDetailDrawer` all hold `detailQuery.data?.etag`, the real captured `ETag` from the same `GET` that populated the edit form. Wiring the fast path through was passing an already-held value one call further, in all five screens, not fetching or deriving anything new. The five gateway wrapper functions (`deleteRoute`/`deleteWebhookEndpoint`/`deleteApp`/`deleteRole`/`deleteUser`) and the five tRPC `remove`/`delete` procedures (`routes.remove`/`webhookEndpoints.remove`/`apps.delete`/`roles.delete`/`users.delete`) each gained the identical optional `etag` pass-through, `z.string().min(1).optional()` on the wire.

**`rest.test.ts`'s `deleteResource` suite was restructured into two describe blocks** — known-version (etag supplied) and no-version-supplied (the fallback) — each with its own 401-retry/stale-`If-Match`/wire-shape coverage, eleven tests in total for this function alone (up from five), the point of each being a call-count assertion: the fast path's own tests assert `fetcher` was called exactly once (no `GET` snuck back in), the fallback's assert exactly two (the `GET` still runs when no version is supplied). 56 tests total in the package now, all passing.

**Verified live against a real gateway (`just demo`), the identical two-direction proof the section above already ran, this time against the fast path itself, over real HTTP with `curl` rather than through the package's own code** — a deliberately more direct proof than re-running the same vitest script, since it exercises nothing but the wire contract `deleteResource` depends on: a real `authorization_code` + PKCE login as the demo's own provisioned `owner` (`POST /login` then `POST /token`, both against the real `sms-gateway`), then:

- **Positive case**: created a throwaway `Route` (`version: 0`), `DELETE`d it with `If-Match: "0"` sent directly and no preceding `GET` at all — `200`, and a follow-up `GET` confirmed a real `404`.
- **Negative case**: created a second throwaway `Route` (`version: 0`), bumped it to `version: 1` with a real concurrent `PATCH` (simulating another operator's edit landing in the fast path's own TOCTOU-free window — the version was captured *before* this write, same as a screen would), then `DELETE`d with the now-stale `If-Match: "0"` — a real `412 PRECONDITION_FAILED`, `"version mismatch: expected 0, found 1"`, and the row confirmed untouched (still `version: 1`) on a follow-up `GET`. Cleaned up with the correct current version afterward.

**Two more instances of this file's own "documentation asserts something the code does not do" pattern were found live, blocking this exact verification — and turned out to be the same two drifts a concurrent PR was independently fixing at the same time.** `compose.dev.yaml` (`just demo`'s own front door) had drifted in two ways since it was last touched, neither caught by anything that runs in CI:

- Its `dockerfile:` paths still pointed at the pre-#271 `app/`/`admin/` locations — the identical bug class #276 already fixed for `release.yml`, just never caught here because `cargo xtask workflow-paths` only checks `.github/workflows/*.yml`, not compose files. Reproduced live: `resolve : lstat /Users/.../app: no such file or directory` on the very first `just demo` attempt for this work.
- `seed-demo-app` still invoked `sms-gateway seed-demo-app`, a subcommand that no longer exists — it was split into its own crate/image, `backends/apps/vsms-demo-seed`, at some point after this file was first written, and `compose.demo.yaml`'s own equivalent service was updated to match (it now pulls `ghcr.io/vymalo/vsms/demo`) while this file's from-source equivalent never was. Reproduced live: `error: unrecognized subcommand 'seed-demo-app'`, with `help`'s own suggestion pointing at `seed-dispatch` instead.

Fixed locally to unblock verification, then discarded from this PR's own diff once #296 ("Fix compose.dev.yaml stale post-rename paths; correct fixed cross-origin comments") landed the same two fixes independently, minutes later — and slightly more completely (it also caught a third stale `admin/server.js` path this PR's own local fix had missed). This PR rebased onto #296 rather than re-deriving the same fix a second time; `compose.dev.yaml` carries no changes here at all now. Worth recording as its own data point for the section below: **two independent sessions found the identical drift live, on the same day, because nothing short of actually running `just demo` surfaces it** — a static check would have caught this once, for everyone, rather than twice, by coincidence of timing.

**That coincidence of timing is also what caused a live infrastructure collision, not a bug in this repo's own code: `compose.dev.yaml` hardcodes a fixed, global Compose project name (`name: vsms-dev`), and the concurrent `just demo` run building PR #296 genuinely raced this one on the same machine** — the exact "container name is fixed and global, don't run overlapping live-test processes" hazard this file's own live-suite section already documents for `sms-test-support`, just for the whole demo stack instead of one test harness's database. That session's `docker compose ... down -v` tore down this session's containers mid-verification (the live gateway became unreachable, `curl: (7) Failed to connect`, between the second and third planned checks). Not a new finding to fix — already an accepted, named risk in this file, just newly confirmed to bite in practice rather than only in theory. The two proofs above (fast-path success, stale-version `412`) both completed before the interference hit; a third, redundant fallback-path live check was dropped rather than re-run, since the fallback path is mechanically the identical `DELETE` + `If-Match` request the two completed proofs already exercised against a real server, preceded only by a `GET` — and `rest.test.ts`'s own fallback-path tests already assert that `GET` genuinely happens with the exact call-count checks described above.

### Checked and found inert, with the reason

- **cratestack#506 (0.7.11, "serialize `Value` untagged on the wire")** — changes how `cratestack_core::Value` serializes (`{"String":"foo"}` → `"foo"`), reaching procedure `Json`-typed args/results, auth claims, and audit-row snapshots. `schema.cstack` never declares a `Json`-scalar field anywhere (every free-form/pre-serialised field in this schema is a plain `String`, per #221's own finding) — checked by grep, not assumed — and the only place this repo reads the framework's own internal `Value` serialization (`cratestack_audit.actor`/`before`/`after`) does so via generic `serde_json::Value` field indexing, agnostic of tag shape. No live database exists anywhere yet (this file's own standing fact), so there's no historical-rows-vs-new-rows shape migration to worry about either.
- **cratestack#509 (0.7.11, "add y -> ies pluralization rule")** — a breaking table-naming change. No model in this schema ends in a consonant + `y` (checked by inspection, and confirmed empirically: the byte-identical DDL diff above proves no table name changed).
- **cratestack#526 (0.7.12, "trust Forwarded/X-Forwarded-For only from a configured proxy")** — `router()`'s own signature gained no new trusted-proxy parameter (only `body_limit_bytes`, above), and this repo's own `RateLimitLayer` construction (#163) deliberately never trusts `X-Forwarded-For` at all — it keys on `ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>` directly, precisely to avoid the spoofing class this cratestack PR closes a different way. Inert by construction.
- **cratestack#542 (0.7.13, "refuse unverifiable idempotency/rate-limit fingerprints instead of pooling them into 'anonymous'")** — changes cratestack's own *default* fingerprint functions for `IdempotencyLayer`/`RateLimitLayer`. `backends/crates/sms-api/src/router.rs` always supplies its own closures (`.with_principal_fingerprint(verified_idempotency_fingerprint)`, `.with_key_fn(client_id_fingerprint)`, `.with_key_fn(source_fingerprint)`) — the defaults are never reached, confirmed by reading every construction site.
- **cratestack#593 (0.7.16, "honour client Accept preference in content negotiation")** — a real RFC 9110 fix, but scoped to the `application/cbor-seq` vs `application/cbor` streaming-RPC path (`select_response_content_type`, `pump_streamed_response_*`). This repo uses `JsonCodec` exclusively, mounts no RPC-streaming transport, and declares no `@stream` procedure anywhere in `schema.cstack` (checked by grep) — single-codec REST negotiation has nothing to order.
- **cratestack#590 (0.7.16, "reject composite `@@id` instead of panicking")** — TypeScript/Dart client codegen only; `schema.cstack` has no composite `@@id` anywhere.
- **cratestack-mock-wiremock (new crate, 0.7.16)** — a new opt-in tool for stateful mocks of a cratestack-generated REST API. Not adopted; this repo's own `wiremock` usage (`sms-provider-orange-cm`, `sms-fake-orange`, the chaos suite) mocks *external* providers (Orange/MTN), an unrelated use case.
- **cratestack#507 (studio direct-DB `@version`/`@@emit` bypass) and cratestack#537 (`@length` on a nullable field breaks codegen) — both closed upstream (0.7.13, PRs [#553](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/553)/[#546](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/546)) and cratestack#567 (PATCH cannot clear a nullable field) closed in 0.7.15 (PR [#574](https://github.com/cratestack/cratestack/pull/574)).** All three were filed by this repo (see `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` §4, updated in this PR) and none is adopted here: `cratestack studio` was already disqualified from any deployed vsms surface for unrelated reasons (`docs/roadmap.md`'s own #46 section); the `AuditAnchor.prevChainHash`/`RouteValidation.notes` `@length` workarounds and `frontends/packages/gateway/src/senders.ts`'s empty-string PATCH-clear sentinel are each a real, separate schema/code change with its own regeneration and re-verification cost, deliberately left for a follow-up rather than bundled into a dependency bump.

### `#176`/`#177` — unchanged by this bump

Neither vsms issue's calculus moved: `grep`ing the six release bodies (v0.7.11 through v0.7.16) for `isSystem`/`upsert`/`do_nothing` returns nothing. Both `auth().isSystem()` (cratestack#500) and `.upsert().do_nothing()` (cratestack#501) landed in cratestack **0.7.10 itself** — already true before this bump — and neither had any further upstream work in the six releases since.

### The SDK and `examples/rust` — verified separately, since no workspace command reaches them

`sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust` and `examples/rust` are both `exclude`d from the root workspace (root `Cargo.toml`) precisely so they can pin independently — which also means `cargo check --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` **never touch either directory**, and neither does any CI job except `cargo xtask sdk-schema-check` (a byte-comparison of the vendored `.cstack` text, which compiles nothing). A bump that left the SDK on the old pin, or broke it outright, would show green everywhere in this repo's own CI. Checked by hand instead: `sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust/Cargo.toml`'s `cratestack = { package = "cratestack-client", ... }` facade (deliberately lighter than `cratestack-pg` — no `axum`/`tower`/`hyper` in its dependency graph, #171) bumped to `=0.7.16`; `cargo check --manifest-path sdks/rust/vsms-sdk-rust/Cargo.toml --all-targets` and `cargo test` (same manifest) both clean (0 unit tests, 3 `live_gateway.rs` tests correctly `#[ignore]`d — they need a running `just demo`, not exercised here); `cargo tree --manifest-path ... -i aws-lc-rs` reports no match, matching the workspace-level guarantee. `examples/rust` depends on the SDK by path (no direct `cratestack` pin of its own) and picks the bump up automatically — `cargo check`/`cargo test --manifest-path examples/rust/Cargo.toml --all-targets` both clean, `aws-lc-rs` absent there too.

### Verification

`just check`/`just lint` (`cargo fmt --check` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`) both clean. `cargo test --workspace` (in-process): 391 passed, 0 failed. **Full live-Postgres suite, foreground, to completion**: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast --tests -- --ignored` — **212 passed, 0 failed**, across all suites this file's own "The live suites run in CI now" section names (including the process-spawning gates: `kill9_reclaim_live.rs`, `m1_acceptance_gate_live_postgres.rs`, `kill_orange_gate_live_postgres.rs`, and `hooks_node_receiver_live.rs`, the last needing a one-time `pnpm install --ignore-workspace --frozen-lockfile` in `examples/node/webhook-receiver` in this environment — a pre-existing local-environment gap this file's own #64 section already names, not a regression). `cargo deny check`: `advisories ok, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok` — one new informational duplicate-version warning (`winnow` 0.7.15/1.0.4, via `cratestack-proto`'s new `toml` dependency), not a `deny.toml` violation. `cargo tree -i aws-lc-rs`: no match, workspace and SDK both — `ring`, still the only provider, unchanged.
